## STATUS 2026-11-14 (newest, latest): LEDGER SIGNATURE D21.1 (UNIVERSAL DESIGN SYSTEM AND APPEARANCE UNIFICATION) APPROVED FOR RELEASE BY OWNER AND CHATGPT AND RELEASED AS `feat(ui): make Ledger Signature the universal appearance` (SHA and CI result in the release summary); NOTHING IS ACC; NEXT PHASE: R0 PRODUCT ASSESSMENT AND STRATEGIC ROADMAP (NOT STARTED)

**What was asked:** make Ledger Signature the one permanent interface (no old-look choice), Signature Navy the default, four more curated palettes, Light/Dark/Device and custom colors kept, a safe transition of saved choices, visual and accessibility verification, report, stop before commit. Gate verified first: D21 is committed and deployed (`582ce64`, run #114 success); only the three protected untracked files present.

**What was built:** Signature structure is now the stylesheet default (`src/ui/tokens.css`); default colors in new `src/ui/palette-base.css`; five palettes (Signature Navy, Coastal Slate, Evergreen, Warm Stone, Midnight Plum) in `src/palette.ts`, colors only, shared status colors; an adapter for custom themes; older ids mapped (Classic/Signature/Deep navy and gold to Signature Navy, Calm teal and Forest green to Evergreen, Slate and indigo to Coastal Slate) by a one-time repeat-safe transition that keeps the old id and deletes nothing; Settings > Appearance rebuilt; browser-bar and manifest colors are Signature Navy's. No schema, backup-format, sync, engine or dependency change.

**Verified:** Signature Navy pixel-identical to the approved prior Signature on 52 of 56 captures (the 4 that differ are the redesigned Appearance page). 288 files, 5,667 tests pass (was 287 / 5,477); `tsc` and build clean; 48 of 48 real-browser checks (Chromium, emulated widths); automated contrast sweep of 21,909 text elements; evidence sheet `Documents\Ledger-Design-Concepts\D21.1-Evidence\sheet.html`. Report: `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D21.1-REPORT.md`.

**Judgment calls (please review):** (1) Mapping of older presets (Calm teal and Forest green to Evergreen; Slate and indigo to Coastal Slate; navy family to Signature Navy). (2) Working colors differ from your starting colors where needed for contrast (darker link colors; Signature Navy keeps its approved `#0E1A33` button). (3) Status colors are shared by all palettes. (4) Starting "My own colors" from a palette now keeps its canvas and card colors. (5) Older pre-paint copies are ignored, so a device that had a non-default palette may show Signature Navy for a moment, once. (6) Superseded Classic-pinning tests were replaced, not relaxed (report 16a). (7) Manifest and bar colors changed to Signature Navy's canvas.

**Final correction (2026-11-14, uncommitted):** Calendar out-of-month, reminder and projected-mark opacity removed (calendar.css only), 0 failing text elements measured in all palettes and modes; 289 files, 5,680 tests pass; ZIP review package in the evidence folder (report section 21).

**NOT built / open:** the "Fix automatically" proposal for unreadable custom themes; removal of dead `var(--x, old)` fallbacks; real-device, screen-reader, forced-colors, Safari and Firefox checks; owner acceptance of anything; the three native `alert()` calls and NC-18, 19 and 22 transactions (still separate, unauthorized).

## STATUS 2026-11-13 (superseded by D21 above; D20.1B is committed and deployed `21a20a1`, run #113 success): LEDGER SIGNATURE D20.1B (THE REMAINING 33 NATIVE CONFIRMATIONS) APPROVED FOR RELEASE BY OWNER AND CHATGPT (release SHA and CI recorded in the release summary); OD-6: ALL 38 MIGRATED, 0 NATIVE confirm() CALLS REMAIN; CF-03 BUILT, AWAITING FINAL OWNER ACCEPTANCE; D21 NOT STARTED

## STATUS 2026-11-13 (newest): D18.2 PERSON-LEVEL GROSS-TO-NET BREAKDOWN APPROVED BY OWNER AND CHATGPT (visual and functional review); RELEASE AUTHORIZED AS `feat(ui): add person-level gross-to-net income breakdown` (SHA, CI and Pages result are recorded in the release summary, not verified when this line was written); D20 AND D21 NOT STARTED

**What was asked:** inside each Income person workspace, replace the basic "At a glance" summary with a gross-to-net statement (income sources, gross, employee deductions, taxes withheld, cash take-home), plus a household comparison under the hero, using one shared set of columns so two people line up position for position; blank cells where a person has no item. **Gate:** T2027 is committed and deployed as `fa5deb7` (Actions run #109 success; `github-pages` deployment `7774563284` success; read from the public GitHub API); `main` = `origin/main`. Report: `docs/LEDGER-SIGNATURE-D18.2-REPORT.md`.

**What was built:** `incomeStatementModel.ts` (the shared column schema and a pure builder that only reads the income model's own `PersonYear` totals), `IncomeStatement.tsx` (`HouseholdComparison`, `PersonStatementView`, read-only), changes to `IncomePage.tsx` and appended `is-` styles in `plan.css`. Cells are `value`, `blank` (empty space with a spoken label, never `$0`) or `unavailable` (Not set up / Not calculated). Deduction categories come from the stored deduction type, not labels. Employer contributions are a separate strip; equity kept as shares is separate from cash. Paycheck detail, forms, RSU and bonus editors are unchanged. **No file in `src/lib`, `src/db.ts`, `src/types.ts`, sync, backup, Search, Quick Add or navigation changed; no dependency added.**

**Tests:** 30 new (`d182IncomeStatement.test.tsx`); 12 files / 199 related tests pass; `tsc` and `npm run build` clean; full suite NOT run locally (CI will). Browser (invented household, own ports, throwaway profile): 1440, 375, 320 in Signature Light, Dark and Classic, no page overflow; statement cell positions (left, top, width) identical across three people; independent expand/collapse; year switch. Pictures: `Documents\Ledger-Design-Concepts\D18.2-Evidence`.

**Judgment calls (please review):**
1. No "Other income" column: the model has no person-owned other income (household Other taxable income stays household-level). An "Other modeled earnings" column appears automatically, in the same place for everyone, only if the model ever produces one.
2. Insurance premiums are one slot (health + dental + vision); FSA is labeled "Health care FSA"; a dependent-care FSA would sit under Other deductions.
3. Withholding categories always show real values once calculated, so Additional Medicare shows `$0.00` for most people.
4. The comparison has nine columns plus a household row; on a phone it becomes labeled lists.
5. Equity kept as shares is shown only when a kept vest exists, under Cash take-home.
6. A kept vest that would be negative loses its shortfall inside the model; the statement would report the difference rather than balance it (not changed, no such case in fixtures). The `keptValue` comment in `payroll.ts` says "dollars" but the value is cents (not changed).

**NOT built / not done:** an Annual / Paycheck switch, a "typical paycheck", editing inside the summary, keyboard Tab walk, screen reader, real phone, forced colors, print, the full local suite. `docs/UI-COVERAGE.md` unchanged (L-17 stays `BLT`). Not released.

## SUPERSEDED (D18.2 is now built, see the status above), recorded 2026-11-13: D18.2 Personal Income Gross-to-Net Breakdown (original proposal)

**Proposed sequence:** D19 -> T2027 -> D18.2 -> D20 -> D21.

**Objective:** inside each existing person-centric Income workspace, present a clear left-to-right breakdown of gross compensation sources, payroll deductions, taxes withheld and estimated cash take-home.

**Requirements to carry forward:**
- Separate regular wages, bonuses and vested RSU compensation without double-counting.
- Show existing payroll deductions and tax withholding by category.
- Keep net RSUs retained as shares separate from cash received.
- Distinguish tax withheld from final household tax liability.
- Preserve existing calculated amounts and all person associations.
- Preserve existing person expand/collapse controls.
- Support responsive desktop and mobile layouts.
- Do not alter payroll engines or database schemas.
- Do not automatically assign household-level other income to a person.

**Pending owner confirmation:** whether the default presentation is annual or per paycheck.

## STATUS 2026-11-13 (superseded by D18.2 above; T2027 is committed and deployed `fa5deb7`, run #109 success, Pages deployment `7774563284` success): T2027 2027 TAX TABLES APPROVED; RELEASE AUTHORIZED AS `feat(tax): add verified 2027 tax tables and year-aware rules` (SHA and CI result in the release summary); Paycheck 2027 browser check passed on fictional data; D18.2, D20 AND D21 NOT STARTED

**What was asked:** research the published 2027 federal and Massachusetts rules and add built-in 2027 tax tables to Income and Financial Planning, using only official sources, without inventing or silently reusing 2026 values. **Gate:** D19 is committed and deployed as `bc353319` (Actions run #108 success; Pages deployment success for the same SHA; read from the public GitHub API); `main` = `origin/main`. Report: `docs/TAX-2027-UPDATE-REPORT.md` (field-by-field table with sources, before and after examples, limitations).

**What was built:** `TAX_2027` with a per-field source record (verified, fixed by law, provisional, planning estimate) shown in a collapsible panel on Income; `RETIREMENT_RULES_2027` and a year lookup so capital-gains thresholds (2027: 0% to $102,100, 15% to $633,600), the 65+ deduction ($1,700) and the 0%-gains room follow the table year; the Roth planner starts from the newest built-in tables not after its first plan year. Federal brackets, standard deduction ($33,200), 401(k) ($25,500 / $8,500 / $11,750), HSA ($4,500 / $9,000), health FSA ($3,500), Social Security base ($192,300) and the MA PFML employee share (0.46%, capped at $192,300) are verified from IRS, SSA and DFML pages. 2026 data was not edited. A saved custom 2027 table still wins over the built-in one.

**Final corrections 2026-11-13 (after ChatGPT review; still uncommitted):** dependent-care FSA reclassified statutory, outdated-saved-2027-table notice added (`taxCustomNotice.test.tsx`, 5 tests), MA surtax wording softened; 83 focused tests pass, `tsc` and build clean; see report section 16.

**Tests:** 34 new tests (`tax2027.test.ts`); 14 targeted files / 237 tests and 23 guard and shell files / 443 tests pass; `tsc` and `npm run build` clean; full suite NOT run locally (CI will). Two older tests that used 2027 as "a year with no tables" now use 2028. Browser check on a throwaway profile with invented data: Income 2026 and 2027, source panel at desktop and 375 px, Roth page, Tax planning 2027, no console errors.

**Judgment calls (please review):**
1. The MA 4% surtax threshold for 2027 is NOT published (DOR lists 2023 to 2026). The 2026 amount ($1,107,750) is carried as a labelled PROVISIONAL placeholder; it is not a 2027 figure and may change when Massachusetts publishes 2027 guidance.
2. The dependent care FSA ($7,500) is STATUTORY (IRS: P.L. 119-21 s.70404, from 2026); corrected from provisional in the final corrections; nothing calculates from it.
3. IRMAA and ACA values for 2027 stay as labelled 2026 carry-forwards (Roth planner only); Rev. Proc. 2026-26 and the $21,640 poverty guideline are leads, not applied.
4. A saved custom 2027 table (for example one copied from 2026 earlier) still wins; since the final corrections Income shows an informational notice when it differs from the built-in table (standard deduction, wage base, brackets, 401(k) limit). Nothing is reset or edited.
5. For a year with no rules of its own (custom tables for 2028 or later) the gains rules now fall back to 2027, not 2026, and Income says so on screen.
6. The IRS Rev. Proc. 2026-35 PDF text still contains a "[Release Date, 2026]" placeholder; all overlapping figures match the IRS news release.

**Separate finding (NOT changed):** the built-in 2026 MA surtax threshold ($1,083,150) is the 2025 figure; DOR lists $1,107,750 for 2026. It only matters above $1.08M of MA taxable income (surtax overstated by at most $984). Recommended as a small separate correction.

**Follow-ups, NOT implemented (decided at review):** (a) correct the 2026 MA surtax threshold from $1,083,150 to $1,107,750 (separate authorized correction); (b) update the 2027 MA surtax threshold when officially published; (c) Roth planner 2027 IRMAA/ACA source verification; (d) README "Every January" tax-table instructions need an eventual update.

**NOT built / not done:** the 2026 surtax correction, IRMAA / ACA 2027 values, MA Circular M 2027 (unpublished), single / head-of-household figures, the CTC / EITC / adoption figures, the Roth catch-up wage threshold ($155,000), a browser check of the Paycheck page for 2027 (unit-tested only), real-device and screen-reader checks. README "Every January" steps not updated.

Headline panel and kind-of-figure words on every page, grouped inputs, results next to or above their inputs (Paycheck, Retirement), a jump list on long pages, labeled rows on a phone, status in words (Done, To do, Within guideline, Looks fine, Needs attention), Guideline versus Recorded separation on Protection, Estimate versus real setup on Paycheck, readable phone charts (`FanChart` opt-in `fit`). New: `PlanParts.tsx`, `plan.css`, `d18Plan.test.tsx` (12 tests). Tests: 568 targeted tests in 32 files pass, `tsc` and the build are clean, the full suite was not run locally. Before and after: 2,803 distinct amounts and 795 input values compared on 33 views, none lost.

**Judgment calls (please review):**
1. Afford forecast and Rent-or-buy charts now plot exact balances, including below zero (they drew $0), like D17's Forecast.
2. Retirement's results panel and the Goals "How it works" card are sticky on a computer; on a phone the Paycheck and Retirement results come before their inputs.
3. Goal settings fold behind a summary line when a goal already has a cost; a new goal opens them.
4. Direction colors removed from refund or amount due, gain or loss and gaps; words carry the meaning.
5. Wording edits: Projections no longer says "the orange steady path"; Retirement's status is a sentence, not a green or red number; Giving reminders start with "Paperwork:"; the Roth winners moved to the top.
6. Phone pages are taller (Income +18%, Roth +17%, Paycheck +22%, Tax planning +51%, Afford +66%, Projections +49%, Retirement +21%, Giving +50%, Protection +59%; Goals -17%).
7. `PercentField` (Retirement confidence) used to show a stale value if the page painted before settings loaded; FIXED in this release (display only, see report section 16).

**ROADMAP:** **D18.1, Person-Centric Income Organization (BUILT AND TESTED LOCALLY, UNCOMMITTED, awaiting review; D18 is deployed as `e423613`; runs before D19).** Sequence: D18 release, then D18.1, then D19, D20, D21. Goal: organize Income around the person each compensation item belongs to instead of household-wide sections by type. Layout: keep the household income summary at the top; one independently expandable workspace per person (several can be open at once) grouping that person's salary and paycheck setup, bonuses, RSU and stock awards, deductions, benefits and contributions; a concise compensation summary in each collapsed header; genuinely household-wide settings (filing assumptions, shared tax settings) stay outside the person containers; ownership of every item is obvious. Safeguards: reuse existing person associations; no automatic reassignment of records; no matching or merging people by name; no change to compensation, RSU, payroll or tax calculations; no schema or migration unless separately justified and approved; vested versus unvested equity keeps its meaning; every existing form, control and record is preserved.

**NOT built / not done:** `Kbd`, contrast, forced colors, real-device, Safari/Firefox and screen-reader checks; every `confirm()` dialog (D20); wider Hide amounts; add, edit and delete flows were preserved by source review and not driven. Nothing is `ACC`.

## STATUS 2026-11-13 (previous, standalone fix): PAYCHECK PAGE CRASH FIXED LOCALLY; UNCOMMITTED, NOT STAGED, NOT PUSHED, NOT DEPLOYED; awaiting owner and ChatGPT review

**Asked:** Plan > Paycheck crashed the app. **Cause:** the page sorted scenarios with `updatedAt.localeCompare`, but sync stamps `updatedAt` as a number, so any saved scenario crashed it (predates D17 and D18). **Fix:** safe `sortScenariosNewestFirst` in `src/lib/paycheckCalc.ts`, used by the page; 3 new tests (paycheckCalc.test.ts 14 pass), tsc and build clean, checked in the app. Full report: `docs/PAYCHECK-CRASH-FIX-REPORT.md`.

**Judgment calls (please review):**
1. I did NOT change `ModuleLoadBoundary`'s deliberate rethrow, so any other screen bug can still freeze the whole app; a small "this screen hit a problem" recovery is proposed in the report for separate approval.
2. Scenarios with a missing or unreadable stamp sort last.

**NOT built / not done:** the recovery boundary, the full test suite (CI will run it), a real-device check. D18 not started.

**Future stabilization item (separate, not a prerequisite for this release):** no page-level crash recovery. `ModuleLoadBoundary` rethrows screen errors and nothing above it catches them, so one broken screen freezes the whole app. Needs its own design, approval and test updates.

## STATUS 2026-11-09: STABILIZATION ST-S5.1 (FINAL SAFETY CORRECTIONS) BUILT AND TESTED LOCALLY. **SUPERSEDED: NOW COMMITTED AND DEPLOYED `ba4da59`, PAGES RUN #79 SUCCESS (verified at the start of F0).** The wording below was written before the commit.

Report: `docs/LEDGER-STABILIZATION-ST-S5-1-REPORT-2026-11-09.md`. Gate: `main` = `4b827eb5e36fea8919708e2f0394d9667b5eda3e` (ST-S4, run #78). Baseline 3,601 in 198; now **3,622 passed in 200 files** (+21 tests: 17 sync, 4 UI), `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, `git diff --check` clean. No schema change (still version 15), no new table, no change to the encrypted sync-file or backup format, no dependency, no financial calculation, known-table merge rules untouched.

- [x] **ST5-F1 (older builds erased newer builds' tables): fixed.** The merge carries any table this build does not have through untouched (`known` list); the engine never remembers ids of unknown tables (and drops any already held); a sync file with a format `v` other than 1 is refused before anything changes. 10 tests with the real SyncEngine (a test-only "newer build" with one extra table): survive repeated older syncs, join, known edits and real deletions still flow, unknown fields on known records survive, upload failure and retry, remote update between retries and during a stale-version race, later upgrade, ids remembered by the previous build, unknown format refused. With the fixes reverted 8 of 10 fail.
- [x] **ST5-F2 (two tabs overwrote each other's conflict list): fixed.** `MetaStore.update` does the read, change and write in ONE IndexedDB transaction (no Web Locks; no weaker fallback); all conflict-list changes use it. 7 tests with two independent connections (control that shows the loss, concurrent adds, add plus remove, clearing an older selection, repeated retries, failed save leaves the list intact, sync stops when the list cannot be saved). Real two-tab check in the built-in browser: old style kept 2 of 120, new kept 120 of 120.
- [x] **ST5-F3** Household Services claim headings are `h3` (were `h4`), computed look identical. **ST5-F4** "All" (and every card link) is at least 44 x 44 on phones, no overflow at 320 and 375, desktop untouched. **Owner request** the module dropdown reads "Household Finances" (verified in the rendered list; route and id unchanged).
- [x] **SE-01 (shared web address): the owner has decided to keep the existing GitHub repositories and GitHub Pages arrangement.** Recorded as a documented, OWNER-ACCEPTED risk; the historical finding and plan are kept. No hosting migration, DNS change, repository transfer or reorganization was proposed or done. It no longer blocks Family Management planning.
- **Judgment calls (please review):** (1) ST5-F2 uses the browser's own all-or-nothing transaction instead of Web Locks (simpler, covers every tab). (2) `MetaStore.update` is required on the interface, so a store that cannot do it fails to compile rather than silently falling back; one test stand-in gained the method. (3) The format guard refuses `v` other than 1; a newer build that changes the format must change `v`. (4) `font-weight: bold` was added to `.sv-h3` so the h3 looks exactly like the old h4. (5) Every phone `.card-link` got the 44 px minimum width, not only "All". (6) Only the dropdown label was capitalised; the page title, Today card, Search entry and Hide Amounts wording still say "Household finances". (7) `ClaimList` is now exported for its test.
- **NOT built / limits:** this protects only devices that run this build (the deployed `4b827eb` still has ST5-F1), so **no database table may be added until this is deployed and installed on every device**; a device that remembered a newer table's ids on the old build and upgrades without syncing on this build could still misread them (only possible once a table exists); older devices never expire unknown tables' deletion marks; two tabs running a whole sync at once were not changed; real phone, second device, real GitHub sync, Safari/Firefox and screen reader are still untested; the populated Services overview was checked by component test and computed styles, not with real data. Family Management NOT started.

Full report: `docs/HOUSEHOLD-SERVICES-S2.1-REPORT-2026-11-06.md`. - [x] **Built:** one guarded Undo for every Services change (new `undo.ts`). When an action finishes it records the whole stored row of everything it touched and the links attached to each; Undo re-checks all of it first and, if anything differs, refuses and writes nothing ("This action can no longer be undone because something it changed has been updated. No changes were made."). Covers Pause, Resume, End, Replace provider, Change schedule, all seven schedule fixes, visit logging, one-off visits, adding an arrangement (form and Quick Add). Undo removes only the links the action made. A token with no recorded state is refused. A contact is removed only by Quick Add Undo, only if untouched and unused by any module. The Undo buttons, previews and dialogs are unchanged; a refusal shows in plain words (toast, or an alert in Quick Add).
- [x] **Timestamp finding:** `updatedAt` is millisecond `Date.now()`; equal stamps with different content are possible (writes in one millisecond; the sync merge sends ties to the other device's copy). The guard compares content, not time. A deterministic test (row rewritten with the stamp preserved) proves it.
- [x] **Tests:** all 11 operations x success and four intervening changes (arrangement edit, Task edit, link on Task, link on arrangement); equal-stamp changed content; unknown fields; set-field ordering; links; contacts; legacy refusal; injected write failures (full rollback, then Undo works); nine real two-device `SyncEngine` scenarios. **Mutation checks** (each restored): state comparison off 34 fail; equal-timestamp detection off 3; link check off 21; refusal bypass 2.

Details: report section 19.
- **Judgment calls (please review):** (1) whole row incl. `updatedAt` is compared, so a same-content re-save refuses; (2) an unrecorded Undo is refused, so one S2 test asserting the old trusting behaviour was rewritten; (3) open visit Tasks shown in the preview are guarded even when the action leaves them alone (needed so "A resumes, B edits the Task" refuses); (4) any new link on a guarded record refuses; (5) only four set fields are order-insensitive (`extraContactIds`, `acknowledged`, `tagIds`, `contactIds`); (6) contact removal stays Quick Add only, in the same transaction (follow-up: an edited created contact now refuses the Undo); (7) Tasks-only repairs do not guard the arrangement; (8) refusal wording; (9) S2 visit Undo is slightly stricter (links); (10) sealing happens inside each action's transaction.

booking Tasks are created from the arrangement page and an existing Task cannot be re-marked (no UI); (2) the next fixed date is the first cadence date after the occurrence dealt with AND the visit date, never before today, so a skipped or cancelled visit dated in the future also passes over that date; (3) "repeat after each visit" counts from min(visit date, today) with Tasks' own `nextDueDate`, also for skipped and cancelled; (4) the Task Tasks spawns is re-dated in place (id and links kept) rather than deleted and recreated, and is removed only when no further visit is due or the arrangement is not active; (5) the next Task's time is the arrangement's standing time, so a one-time time change does not carry forward; (6) logging does not rewrite the arrangement record (the `openTaskId` hint is left alone; the link is the truth), which avoids cross-device conflicts on it; (7) logging one of two open duplicate Tasks leaves the other, so the duplicate-open notice remains; (8) duplicate detection also groups the same cadence date from different Tasks and same-day hand-logged visits, so a deliberately logged stale copy can be flagged; - **Judgment calls (please review):** (1) any live OPEN Task linked (type `about`) to an arrangement counts as its visit task, so manually linking a second open Task shows "duplicate-open" (which can be left as it is); (2) health priority is no-task, duplicate-open, rule-differs, stale-marker, off-cadence, moved-once (stale before moved-once so a Task finished in Tasks and respawned is not mislabelled as a harmless move); (3) stale-marker is detected in S1 from finished linked Tasks' markers because visit records do not exist until S2; (4) a cadence may not end after a number of times (a count shrinks as Tasks spawn the next copy and would show false drift), use an end date; (5) "leave it as it is" is remembered on the arrangement as small keys (`acknowledged`) that stop matching when the Task rule changes; (6) an extra `visitTime` field holds the clock time written to the Task's due time; (7) Replace provider copies title, schedule, cost estimate and linked assets, but NOT notes (unless ticked), document links or other contacts, because those can hold the old provider's private details; (8) Pause and End default to removing the visit Task (with Undo), Replace defaults to "keep this one visit, stop it repeating"; (9) Resume reuses a kept Task and only makes it follow the schedule if the owner ticks the box, otherwise a new Task takes the first schedule date on or after today and after any visit already finished (no catch-up); (10) Edit cannot change an existing provider (use Replace) but can assign one when there is none; (11) there is no delete button for an arrangement in S1 (End it instead); Undo on Quick Add or on the add form removes the new arrangement and its Task; (12) the Task title is "Title: Provider name" and carries nothing else; (13) the cost estimate honours Today's "Hide amounts" switch by reading its stored setting; (14) an arrangement with no schedule can have one visit scheduled on a chosen date, as a Task that does not repeat; (15) Quick Add grammar: repeat, date and time use the Tasks shorthand and the words after the last " with " are the provider.

## STATUS 2026-11-06 (earlier): HOUSEHOLD SERVICES S0 REVISION 2 WRITTEN (after ChatGPT architectural review). DESIGN ONLY: NOTHING BUILT, COMMITTED OR PUSHED. Architecture approved in principle; Q1 to Q9 accepted as planning defaults; Revision 2 later received final approval and S1 was authorized and built (entry above).

**Revision 2 changes (plan section 0):** (1) scheduling authority: the arrangement owns the intended cadence (with an anchor date) and Tasks execute it; a reschedule moves ONE visit only; Services computes the next due date from the cadence anchor at "Done, log it" (S2); drift between Task and cadence is detected (`scheduleHealth`: no-task, duplicate-open, rule-differs, off-cadence, moved-once, stale-marker) and fixed only by explicit, previewed choices; `ensureNextVisitTask` keeps exactly one open Task through pause/resume/replace; concurrent-device duplicates are detected, not auto-deleted; a timed visit is the Task with a due time and **no Calendar event in S1 or S2**; no second recurrence engine; targeted S1/S2 regression lists (plan 6.10). (2) Visits: completed / skipped / cancelled are different facts; cancelled visits may carry a legitimate fee (`chargedCents` + `chargeKind`) and a payment, are never service delivered, and a Finance payment never proves work happened. (3) `accountNumber` removed from S1; provider account identifiers deferred pending a use case and privacy review; notes still must not hold codes, passwords or bank instructions. (4) `householdHomeSpend` fully specified (plan 7.6): claims, global whole-supersedes-parts, dedupe by `pieceKey`, `recorded-as` pair rule, typed amounts never matched by value, refunds, review claims, shared payments never apportioned, module-versus-household reconciliation identity, never an allocation. (5) S1 links an arrangement to an existing asset from the Services UI only; all reverse Home & Assets UI moves to S4.
**Revision 2 judgment calls (please review):** reschedule = one visit; cadence-anchored next date; drift never silently repaired; no Calendar event before a fresh owner decision; linked-pair typed amount favours no double counting (flagged on screen); household total never allocates.
**Still unverified:** reschedule/parity behaviour and that the Task editor preserves unknown fields are read from code, not executed (S1/S2 tests); nothing tested on real devices.

## DECISION FOR LATER (2026-11-04): push notifications to iPhone / Apple Watch. NOT being built now.

What it would take (from general knowledge of Apple's rules; nothing here was tested on the owner's devices):
- Ledger installed to the iPhone Home Screen (iOS 16.4 or newer), a small install file and background script, and a one-time permission prompt.
- Something always on that sends the notification at the due time (the app is a static page with no server): a free Cloudflare Worker on a timer is the likeliest fit; a GitHub Actions timer can run many minutes late. It also needs the phone's notification address and a list of what is due (read from the synced data, or handed over by the app at each sync).
- Privacy: notification text passes through Apple's servers. Keep it vague ("Task due: ...") and send only titles and times, never amounts or balances.
- Apple Watch needs nothing extra: iPhone notifications mirror to it when the phone is locked or not in use. No Watch screen for Ledger.
- Cheaper routes: (a) calendar alerts via the existing .ics alarms (iPhone Calendar and Watch alert natively; one-time import, so changes do not carry over unless a live calendar subscription is built, which Phase 2 excluded); (b) a notification app such as Pushover or ntfy.
- Recommendation: try the calendar-alert route first; build real push later only if wanted, as its own phase of roughly Part B's size.

- **Tasks on a project** (optional `projectId` on the existing task record): a Tasks section on the project page (add, add an existing one, tick, edit in the normal Task editor, remove from the project without deleting, To do / Overdue / Done / All, progress); a Project box in the Task editor; a project tag on task rows; a project filter on the Tasks page.
- **Milestones** (a task with `isMilestone`, no table of its own): a Milestones section with due date, tick, reopen, progress and overdue count; never double-counted with ordinary tasks; they reach the Calendar through the existing Tasks layer (filled-diamond title), no extra event.
- **Events on a project** (optional `projectId` on the existing event record, whole series for a repeating one): an Events section (add via the normal Calendar editor, add an existing one, remove, open in Calendar); a Project box in the event editor; a project marker on Agenda rows, day lists, tooltips and the detail dialog.
- **Project dates:** a read-only **Projects** calendar layer with each active project's start and target date (past ones are "handled", never overdue; a target marker is left out on a day when an open milestone of that project is due).
- **Coming up** list on the project page (next 30 days: tasks, milestones, events, plus overdue).
- **Today card "Projects":** active (Planning or Active) projects with overdue milestones or tasks, a target past or within 14 days; counts only, so no task or event shows twice.
- **Finance:** a Project filter on Transactions (chip, `?project=<id>`) and `?txn=<id>` to open one transaction; each project transaction row links to it, and "See in Finance".
- **Multi-line commitment allocation: design review written, not built.**

**Judgment calls (please review):** see section 8 of the report. In short: (1) Today shows only Planning and Active projects, 14-day target window; (2) a project's target marker and a same-day milestone are both shown (owner review); (3) past markers are "handled", not overdue; (4) one project per task or event, a repeating event's project covers the series; (5) linking something already on another project moves it, with Undo; (6) a repeating task cannot be made a milestone (enforced in the editor and `setMilestone`, not in the low-level repeat, because a Phase 0 test requires unknown fields to pass to the next repeat); (7) two existing tests that counted the built-in calendars were updated from 3 to 4.

**NOT built:** the multi-line allocation itself (P4); a project word in the Add box's task shorthand; bulk "move to project" in Tasks; project name in search result lines; project counts on the Projects list; a project filter in Reports or Calendar; file or document storage. **Not verified:** offline use and two real devices syncing; in a browser, changing or removing a project in the Calendar editor, the Milestone box in the Task editor, and dark theme.

## STATUS 2026-11-05: P2 FOLLOW-UPS FROM THE OWNER'S REVIEW BUILT, COMMITTED (ba2021e) AND PUSHED 2026-11-05 with P2 (9928eb9). 1,206 tests pass (was 1,194). P3 is NOT started.

1. **Split a payment by contract AND budget line.** Example: one $10k payment to a general contractor who coordinates the subs: $5k tile, $3k plumbing, $2k electrical, all counting toward the same project. Each piece of a split (and the whole-transaction box) now picks project, then optionally a contract, then optionally a budget line. Every piece on the contract counts against its "Left to pay"; each line gets its own piece. The Finance layer already read "the piece's own line, else the contract's line", so the change is in the form rules (`buildRef`) and the box (`ProjectTag`); the stored shape did not change (no schema, backup or sync change). The project's transaction list now shows "contract · line".
2. **N key opens the Add box** from anywhere (`GlobalFrame`), ignored while typing, with Ctrl/Cmd/Alt held, while a dialog is open, or when the key is held down. The Add button's tooltip says "Add (N)"; listed in More > Shortcuts and the README.

Tests added: 7 (split by contract and line; part payment; contract filed under a line; overpayment across parts; two contracts in one payment; wrong-project and money-back-on-a-part refusals; round trip through the form) and 5 (the N key rules). One older test changed meaning: a contract's own line is still not stored twice, but a DIFFERENT line is now kept.

Checked in a real browser (invented data, desktop and 375px, no console errors): N opened the Add box and typed nothing into it; Ctrl+N did nothing; a split of three pieces to one contract with three different lines saved through the form (this also covers the earlier unchecked "saving a tagged split through the form"), reopened with every tag intact, and the project page showed the contract paid for the whole amount with each line's share; no sideways scroll at 375px; the line list shows "Same as the contract (X)" first when the contract is filed under a line.

Judgment calls (please review):
1. A contract still has ONE home line (or none). Payments can go to other lines, but the contract's "Agreed" and "Left to pay" stay on its own line. For a general contractor, leave the contract with NO line: its agreed and left-to-pay then sit in the "No budget line" row, and the dashboard will say that amount is "not filed under any budget line". Splitting the agreed amount itself across lines is NOT built.
2. The same rule applies to the whole-transaction Project box (not only split pieces): a single payment can name a contract and a different line.
3. Money coming back on a split piece with a contract must still be marked reversal or credit (same rule as before).
4. The N key is ignored whenever any dialog is open, including a form in a dialog.
5. Bulk tagging still cannot set a contract line or split anything (unchanged).

NOT built: splitting a contract's agreed amount across lines; a contract picker that knows how much of each sub's quote remains; shortcuts for other single-key actions.

Remove takes it off this project only. The same contact can be on several projects.

## DECISION 2026-11-05 (owner): refunds versus credits in Projects. DECIDED AND BUILT into the project layer (uncommitted, with Phase 0). 1,108 tests pass.

The owner asked that money coming back be split into a **reversal** (undoes a payment) and a **credit** (reduces the contract), and that this be settled before the Projects dashboard is built. This replaces the single refund rule (plan edge case 7 and decision O2).

| Kind | Example | Agreed | Paid | Left to pay |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Reversal | bounced cheque, duplicate charge returned, deposit returned to be paid again | unchanged | down | goes back up |
| Credit | discount, damaged item, scope cut | down, by a negative change order | down only if cash comes back | agreed minus paid, never below zero |
| Other money in (no contract) | insurance payout, rebate | no change | down | no change |

A credit applied to the balance has no transaction at all: it is only the negative change order.

**Owner's test case (becomes a test):** accept a $10,000 contract (paid $0, outstanding $10,000); pay a $3,000 deposit (paid $3,000, outstanding $7,000); the deposit is refunded and the contract stays active, marked Reversal (paid $0, outstanding must go back to $10,000). Under the old single refund rule outstanding would have stayed $7,000, which is wrong. Also test the same refund marked Credit with a -$3,000 change order (agreed $7,000, paid $0, outstanding $7,000).

Owner answers: (1) a refund tagged to a contract **must be marked** Reversal or Credit before it can be saved (no default); (2) marking a credit **offers a pre-filled negative change order** for the same amount, which can be unticked; if skipped, the dashboard flags "credit of $X has no matching reduction in the agreed amount".

**What was changed (done):** `ProjectRef` gets an optional `effect: 'reversal' | 'credit'`, used only on money-in tagged to a contract; `summariseProject` subtracts both reversals and credits from what was paid on the contract (so a credit with no matching negative change order makes the contract look owed again); new flags `creditsNotReflected` (credit with no matching reduction) and `unmarkedRefunds` (old or foreign data); `setTxnProject`, `bulkSetTxnProject` (counts `needsChoice`) and `createTxn` refuse money-in tagged to a contract with no mark, and refuse a mark on a payment or without a contract; the owner's $10,000 case, the credit version, a credit with and without its change order, and the database paths are tests; plan updated (edge case 7, rules, example 1 step 7, R5, O2). Not built: the "must choose" prompt and the offered change order (Projects screens). The "must choose" prompt and the offered change order belong to the Projects screens.

**Follow-up fix after the post-implementation check (uncommitted):** money coming back on a contract can no longer be more than what was paid on it. `setTxnProject`, `bulkSetTxnProject` (counts `exceedsPayments`) and `createTxn` refuse it with "That is more than the $X paid on this contract so far"; the summary has a `reversalsExceedPayments` flag and a `paidByCommitment` figure (never below zero) for old or synced-in data. A partial reversal of a partial payment is tested ($3,000 paid, $1,000 reversed: paid $2,000, outstanding $8,000 on a $10,000 contract). Two existing new tests were changed because they tagged a refund with nothing paid yet. 1,111 tests pass. Still a Projects-screen job: ask "is this refund about a contract?" when money coming back is tagged to a project only.

**What was built (numbers match the queue below):**

Did NOT change the backup code to build its lists automatically (offered as a later step).
2. **Unknown fields kept** on tasks and events (`asTask`, `asEvent` now carry fields they do not know). Tests (`src/modules/tasks/unknownFields.test.ts`): survive edit, bulk change, complete, reopen, repeat-spawn, event save; clearing a known field still clears.
3. **Finance project layer, no screens** (`src/lib/txnProject.ts`, `ProjectRef` on `Txn` and `Split` in `src/types.ts`): tag and untag a transaction or one split, bulk tag, refusals (transfers, setup entries, wrong project's line or commitment), tag cleared when a transaction becomes a transfer or is paired, bulk category change reports dropped split tags, and `summariseProject` / `loadProjectSummary` implementing the rules in the plan (change orders roll into their contract; refunds never re-open what is owed). The three worked examples from the plan are tests and match to the cent; reports and budget give identical results with and without tags; the field survives backup, restore and sync. No database version change, no index.
4. **Date fixes**: `asEvent` default date, Calendar search fallback, encrypted export filename now use the local day. `dateZones.test.ts` (Los Angeles, Auckland, UTC) and `dateGuard.test.ts` (fails if a new UTC-day slice appears outside five allowed files).
5. **Shared code moved**: `time` and `recurrence` to `src/core/`, priority names to `src/core/priority.ts`, `pickers`, `RepeatFields`, `PriorityMark` to `src/ui/`. One-line re-exports remain at the old Calendar paths. Tasks, Today and Ledger now import from the new places. CSS and the Calendar tests were not moved.
6. **Linkable Tasks and Calendar events** (`records.ts` in each): the "Link a record" picker now offers Ledger, Calendar and Tasks; a deleted target shows "(no longer exists)".
7. **Sync visibility**: Sync page shows file size, record count, uploads today and in 30 days, and a note past 5 MB. Automatic runs skip the download when GitHub's file is the version this device last saw and nothing changed here (Sync now always does a full run). Tests in `src/lib/sync/skip.test.ts`.
8. **Security policy** added to the production build only (`src/shell/csp.ts`, plugin in `vite.config.ts`): this site only, GitHub and the price service for connections, two inline theme scripts allowed by hash. A test fails if the app fetches from a host not on the list.

**Checked in a real browser (desktop and 375 px, invented data, then removed):** Tasks, Calendar, Today open with no errors and no sideways scroll; the task editor shows the repeat box and the Link picker with Ledger, Calendar and Tasks; a transaction added through the quick Add box changed the balance correctly. On the production build the policy is in place, a connection to a non-listed host was blocked, and the dark-theme setting still applied (so the allowed inline script runs).

**NOT verified (honest list):** the offline service worker under the new policy (the preview browser cannot register a service worker even without the policy, so this needs your phone or Chrome after publishing); real GitHub sync with the skip logic and the new Sync page figures (tests use an in-memory copy only); two real devices; phone speed; the "More options" transaction form and the edit form were not opened after the create-path change (type-checked and unit-tested only); price lookups and sync under the policy (hosts are on the list; not exercised).

**Judgment calls (please review):**
1. Unknown fields are carried in `asTask`/`asEvent` (not merged at save time), so every writer, including spawn and bulk, keeps them. Calendar layers and task lists were not changed.
2. A transaction tag is one small object (`project`, optional `budgetLineId`, `commitmentId`) on the transaction and on each split; a split's own tag wins. Splits with no tag inherit the transaction's.
3. A change order is a commitment with `parentId`; payments tagged to the original or the change order count toward the whole contract. Payments on a quoted, declined or cancelled contract appear as "paid on inactive" and still count as paid.
4. "Expected total" ignores a contract's overpayment (shown as a flag only).
5. The skip check treats any changed record, any change in record counts, or any record without a timestamp as "changed here" and falls back to a full run. Device-only price key changes are ignored.
6. The policy allows inline styles (`'unsafe-inline'` for styles only) because screens set styles directly.
7. The Calendar tests stayed where they were and import through the old paths.
8. No test for `headSha` in the GitHub client itself (the listing code was refactored and the existing GitHub tests still pass).

**NOT built / still queued:** deriving the backup lists from the database automatically; compressing the weekly encrypted backup; passphrase hint (plan item B7, not in the nine); Projects itself (its own plan: record types, screens, dashboard, transaction "Project" field, Today card, Search, Add, vendor details on contacts); owner actions (GitHub two-step sign-in, token check, other-Pages-sites check, optional two-device test).

## STATUS 2026-11-05: Phase 3 PART E BUILT (verification and tuning). SIGNED OFF 2026-11-05 (all final checklist items passed, including the open Part A, C and D steps), committed and pushed. PHASE 3 IS COMPLETE. Phase 4 is NOT started.

Owner decisions: heavy invented data; also replace the remaining browser suggestion pop-ups; fix only obvious speed problems.

**What was done (Part E):**
- **Speed measured** (desktop browser, no slowdown, 20,000 transactions + 1,500 tasks + 600 events + 150 scheduled items, switching screens inside the app; 4 runs each; 'settled' = nothing on screen changed for 0.35 s; 'longest block' = the longest time the page was frozen):
  - Calendar: first content 0.15 to 0.3 s, settled about 0.25 s, longest block about 0.1 to 0.14 s.
  - Tasks: first content 0.1 to 0.2 s, settled 0.1 to 0.2 s, longest block 0.06 to 0.13 s.
  - Ledger Home: first content 0.03 to 0.5 s, settled about 0.9 to 1.0 s, longest block 0.13 to 0.21 s.
  - Today BEFORE the fix: settled 0.95 to 1.5 s, longest block 0.49 to 0.69 s, about 9,700 page elements.
  - Today AFTER the fix: first content 0.03 to 0.24 s, settled 0.57 to 0.87 s, longest block 0.16 to 0.23 s, about 2,000 elements.
  - Search (from Part C, same data): about 25 ms per search, about 130 ms from typing to results.
- **The one obvious problem, fixed:** with that much data, Today's Overdue card drew 643 rows at once. It now lists the first rows only (a shared setting: **Edit layout > Overdue lists up to 5, 10, 15, 20 or 25 items**, default 25, next to the look-ahead setting) and, beyond that, shows a link per module such as "Show 35 more overdue items in Tasks" that opens the module's own list (Tasks: Overdue view; Ledger: Scheduled; Calendar: Agenda from the start of the overdue window). The count badge always shows the true total. Checked in a real browser with 40 invented overdue tasks (set to 25: 25 rows and "Show 15 more"; set to 5: 5 rows and "Show 35 more"); removed again.
- **Slowest remaining part** is about 0.6 to 0.9 s of waiting for the cash forecast (about 0.18 s of it) and alerts to finish over 20,000 transactions; Ledger Home does the same work. Not changed (proposal: none needed unless it feels slow on your phone).
- **Widths:** at 700 px (the 641 to 899 range) and at 320 px (narrow phone), Today, Calendar, Tasks and Ledger have no sideways scroll; the round + sits in the bottom corner (above the tab bar in Ledger) and nothing was under it; the search dialog and Add box fit the screen.
- **Real backup and restore, run in the browser:** built a 5.3 MB backup of the heavy data, deleted 63 transactions and changed the Today layout and Hide amounts settings, then restored: all 20,009 transactions came back and both settings came back exactly. 
- **Pop-ups replaced:** the giving charity box, the investment ticker box and the RSU grant ticker box now use the in-app suggestion list (like the payee box). No browser pop-up lists remain in the app.
- Test data was all removed again (the browser is back to its original counts).

**Verified:** 1,046 tests, type check and production build clean.

**NOT verified (honest list):** speed on a phone-speed processor (my machine has no slowdown, and the preview tool cannot throttle it; your phone is the real test); the round + on a real iPhone; two real devices syncing; midnight rollover (see steps below); the new suggestion list on the Investments and RSU grant screens (type-checked, and the Giving screen was opened in a browser, but I did not open an investment account or the grant form); Today with 30-day look-ahead on heavy data.

**Judgment calls (please review):**
1. Overdue lists 25 rows by default (your choice of 5 to 25 in Edit layout). The "more" link goes to each module's own screen rather than expanding in place. The Calendar link opens the Agenda from the start of the overdue window, which shows only the next 14 days from there, so it may not list every Calendar item (Calendar events are rarely the overdue ones).
2. Only Overdue is capped. Coming up (up to 30 days) and Today's own list are not; they were 105 and 35 rows in the test.
3. I did not slow the processor, so I cannot say how a phone will feel; the numbers above are a desktop baseline.
4. The ticker boxes list tickers only (the company name is not shown next to each suggestion any more).

**Deferred or skipped, and why:**
- Phone-speed measurement: needs a real device.
- Two-device and midnight checks: need your real devices (steps below).
- A cap or collapse for the other cards: not needed on these numbers.
- Ledger fields for card due days and subscriptions, and real push notifications (see the notes at the top of this file): later phase.
- Part D item still open from you: re-test steps 3, 4, 5, 7, 9 and 11.

**Final Phase 3 checklist (for the owner):**
Part E items
1. Open Today on your real data, on desktop and phone. Does it feel quick? Note anything slow and on which device. Overdue lists up to the number you choose in Edit layout (5, 10, 15, 20 or 25); if there are more, a link such as "Show N more overdue items in Tasks" opens that module's own list. Try changing the number.
